# Multiple Correspondence Analysis

A user-friendly wrapper around
[`MCA`](https://rdrr.io/pkg/FactoMineR/man/MCA.html), made to work with
ggfacto functions like
[`ggmca`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/ggmca.md),
[`interpret`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/interpret.md)
and
[`hierarchical_clust`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/hierarchical_clust.md).
Variables are selected the way of the \`tidyverse\`, as in
[`tabxplor::tab()`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/tabxplor/reference/tab.html).
Supplementary variables are not given here: they are added afterwards,
in [`ggmca`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/ggmca.md).

\`MCA2()\` keeps the fit of ggfacto 0.3.2, on the individuals: \`\$ind\`
has one row per analysed row, so that \`FactoMineR::HCPC()\` of it
classifies the individuals, in their order. It gives the same graphs,
tables and clusters as \`multiple_correspondence_analysis()\`, more
slowly on large data, and will be deprecated.

## Usage

``` r
multiple_correspondence_analysis(
  data,
  active_vars,
  wt,
  excl = NA,
  ncp = Inf,
  graph = FALSE,
  filter,
  ...
)

MCA2(data, active_vars, wt, excl = NA, ncp = Inf, graph = FALSE, filter, ...)
```

## Arguments

- data:

  The data frame. To analyse a subset of the population, give the whole
  data frame and \`filter\`, or filter it inside the call with the
  native pipe, \`data \|\> dplyr::filter(...) \|\>
  multiple_correspondence_analysis(...)\`: the analysis then remembers
  which rows it used, so that
  [`ggmca`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/ggmca.md),
  [`hierarchical_clust`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/hierarchical_clust.md)
  or
  [`is_in_analysis`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/is_in_analysis.md)
  can be given the whole data frame afterwards.

- active_vars:

  \<[tidy-select](https://tidyr.tidyverse.org/reference/tidyr_tidy_select.html)\>
  The active variables.

- wt:

  \<[tidy-select](https://tidyr.tidyverse.org/reference/tidyr_tidy_select.html)\>
  The weight variable, if any.

- excl:

  The levels to exclude from the calculation of the axes (specific
  multiple correspondence analysis), matched exactly by name. The
  missing values of each active variable become a level named
  \`\<VAR\>.NA\`, and \`NA\`, the default, excludes all of them: \`excl
  = NA\` for missing values only, \`excl = c(NA, "Other")\` to exclude a
  level too, \`excl = "DIPLOMA.NA"\` for the missing values of one
  variable only, \`excl = NULL\` to keep every level.

- ncp:

  The number of axes to keep. All of them by default: the eigenvalue
  table is how one chooses how many axes to interpret, and a truncated
  one cannot show the drop — it also renormalises Benzecri's modified
  rate over the axes it kept, so the same axis gets a different rate. To
  cluster on the first axes, give
  [`hierarchical_clust`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/hierarchical_clust.md)
  its own \`ncp\`.

- graph:

  By default no graph is made, since the result can be plotted with
  [`ggfacto`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/ggfacto.md).

- filter:

  A condition on the rows of \`data\`, as in
  [`dplyr::filter()`](https://dplyr.tidyverse.org/reference/filter.html):
  only the rows where it is \`TRUE\` are analysed (\`filter = AGE \>=
  18\`).

- ...:

  Additional arguments to pass to
  [`MCA`](https://rdrr.io/pkg/FactoMineR/man/MCA.html), except those
  that index its rows or columns (\`ind.sup\`, \`quali.sup\`,
  \`quanti.sup\`, \`tab.disj\`).

## Value

A \`MCA\` object from FactoMineR, fitted on the distinct answer profiles
(the combinations of active answers), each weighted by its individuals:
the eigenvalues and every result on the levels are the individuals', and
\`\$ind\` has one row per profile (per individual with \`MCA2()\`). Use
[`axis_coord`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/axis_coord.md)
and
[`hierarchical_clust`](https://bricenocenti.github.io/ggfacto/reference/hierarchical_clust.md)
to write coordinates and clusters into the data frame
(\`FactoMineR::HCPC()\` would cluster the profiles). One more element,
\`source\`, records for each row of \`data\` its row of \`\$ind\`
(\`NA\` if it was not analysed) and its weight.

## Examples

``` r
data(tea, package = "FactoMineR")
res.mca <- multiple_correspondence_analysis(tea, 1:18)
interpret(res.mca)                           # the eigenvalues, then the axes

  dplyr::filter(age < 30) |>
  multiple_correspondence_analysis(1:18)
# the same analysis
res.mca_young <- multiple_correspondence_analysis(tea, 1:18, filter = age < 30)
```
